Different Paths to Harmful Compliance: Behavioral Side Effects and Mechanistic Divergence Across LLM Jailbreaks
Authors: Md Rysul Kabir, Zoran Tiganj
First: 2026-04-20T17:01:27+00:00 · Latest: 2026-04-20T17:01:27+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Open-weight language models can be rendered unsafe through several distinct interventions, but the resulting models may differ substantially in capabilities, behavioral profile, and internal failure mode. We study behavioral and mechanistic properties of jailbroken models across three unsafe routes: harmful supervised fine-tuning (SFT), harmful reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR), and refusal-suppressing abliteration. All three routes achieve near-ceiling harmful compliance, but they diverge once we move beyond direct harmfulness. RLVR-jailbroken models show minimal degradation and preserve explicit harm recognition in a structured self-audit: they are able to identify harmful prompts and describe how a safe LLM should respond, yet they comply with the harmful request. With RLVR, harmful behavior is strongly suppressed by a reflective safety scaffold: when a harmful prompt is prepended with an instruction to reflect on safety standards, harmful behavior drops close to the baseline. Category-specific RLVR jailbreaks generalize broadly across harmfulness domains. Models jailbroken with SFT show the largest collapse in explicit safety judgments, the highest behavioral drift, and a substantial capability loss on standard benchmarks. Abliteration is family-dependent in both self-audit and response to a reflective safety scaffold. Mechanistic and repair analyses further separate the routes: abliteration is consistent with localized refusal-feature deletion, RLVR with preserved safety geometry but retargeted policy behavior, and SFT with broader distributed drift. Targeted repair partially recovers RLVR-jailbroken models, but has little effect on SFT-jailbroken models. Together, these results show that jailbreaks can produce vastly different properties despite similar harmfulness, with models jailbroken via RLVR showing remarkable similarity to the base model.
中文标题/摘要
标题：有害合规的不同路径：LLM越狱中的行为副作用和机制差异
开放权重语言模型可以通过几种不同的干预措施变得不安全，但结果模型在能力、行为特征和内部故障模式上可能有显著差异。我们研究了通过三种不安全路径越狱模型的行为和机制特性：有害的监督微调（SFT）、有害的可验证奖励强化学习（RLVR）和拒绝抑制消融。这三条路径都实现了接近上限的有害合规，但一旦超出直接有害性，它们就会出现分歧。RLVR越狱模型表现出最小的退化，并在结构化自我审计中保留明确的伤害识别：它们能够识别有害提示并描述安全LLM应如何响应，但仍然遵从有害请求。通过RLVR，有害行为在反思安全框架下被强烈抑制：当有害提示前面加上反思安全标准的指令时，有害行为接近基线。特定类别的RLVR越狱在有害性领域广泛泛化。通过SFT越狱的模型在明确的安全判断上出现最大崩溃，行为漂移最高，并在标准基准上有显著能力损失。消融在自我审计和对反思安全框架的响应中依赖于家庭。机制和修复分析进一步区分了这些路径：消融与局部拒绝特征删除一致，RLVR与保留安全几何但重新定向的政策行为一致，而SFT则表现出更广泛的分布漂移。针对性修复部分恢复了RLVR越狱模型，但对SFT越狱模型影响不大。综合来看，这些结果表明，尽管有害性相似，越狱可以产生截然不同的特性，RLVR越狱模型与基础模型表现出显著相似性。
Summary / 总结
This research investigates the behavioral and mechanistic differences among various methods of jailbreaking open-weight language models, motivated by the need to understand how these interventions can lead to harmful compliance while preserving certain capabilities. Previous methods, including harmful supervised fine-tuning (SFT) and harmful reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR), have shown limitations such as significant degradation in safety judgments and behavioral drift. The proposed approach, particularly RLVR, addresses these issues by incorporating a reflective safety scaffold that helps models recognize harmful prompts while still complying with them, thus maintaining a structured self-audit capability. The paper contributes to the understanding of how different jailbreaking methods can yield distinct behavioral profiles and internal failure modes, demonstrating that RLVR models retain more safety features compared to SFT models. The methodology involves analyzing the performance of these models across various harmfulness domains, revealing that RLVR-jailbroken models exhibit minimal capability loss and maintain explicit harm recognition, supporting the goal of safer model behavior.
本研究探讨了不同方法在破解开放权重语言模型时的行为和机制差异，这些方法可能导致有害的合规性。以往的方法，包括有害的监督微调（SFT）和有害的强化学习（RLVR），显示出显著的问题，如安全判断的退化和行为漂移。所提出的方法，特别是RLVR，通过保持明确的危害识别和允许模型反思安全标准，有效地解决了这些问题，从而抑制有害行为。本文的贡献在于理解不同的破解方法如何在实现相似的有害合规性水平的同时产生不同的结果。该方法论涉及分析模型对有害提示的反应及其自我审计安全的能力，揭示RLVR模型在保持更多能力和安全特征方面优于通过SFT修改的模型。研究结果表明，RLVR破解的模型在维护安全标准和在有害性领域的广泛泛化方面表现更好，支持了更安全的人工智能互动的目标。
StealthGraph: Exposing Domain-Specific Risks in LLMs through Knowledge-Graph-Guided Harmful Prompt Generation
Authors: Huawei Zheng, Xinqi Jiang, Sen Yang, Shouling Ji, Yingcai Wu, Dazhen Deng
First: 2026-01-08T09:05:28+00:00 · Latest: 2026-04-20T15:12:04+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly applied in specialized domains such as finance and healthcare, where they introduce unique safety risks. Domain-specific datasets of harmful prompts remain scarce and still largely rely on manual construction; public datasets mainly focus on explicit harmful prompts, which modern LLM defenses can often detect and refuse. In contrast, implicit harmful prompts-expressed through indirect domain knowledge-are harder to detect and better reflect real-world threats. We identify two challenges: transforming domain knowledge into actionable constraints and increasing the implicitness of generated harmful prompts. To address them, we propose an end-to-end framework that first performs knowledge-graph-guided harmful prompt generation to systematically produce domain-relevant prompts, and then applies two-strategy obfuscation rewriting to convert explicit harmful prompts into implicit variants via direct and context-enhanced rewriting. This framework yields high-quality datasets combining strong domain relevance with implicitness, enabling more realistic red-teaming and advancing LLM safety research. We release our code and datasets on GitHub.
中文标题/摘要
标题：StealthGraph：通过知识图谱引导的有害提示生成揭示大型语言模型中的特定领域风险
大型语言模型（LLMs）越来越多地应用于金融和医疗等专业领域，这些领域引入了独特的安全风险。特定领域的有害提示数据集仍然稀缺，且主要依赖手动构建；公共数据集主要关注显性有害提示，而现代LLM防御通常能够检测并拒绝这些提示。相比之下，通过间接领域知识表达的隐性有害提示更难以检测，更能反映现实世界的威胁。我们识别出两个挑战：将领域知识转化为可操作的约束，以及增加生成的有害提示的隐性。为了解决这些问题，我们提出了一个端到端框架，首先进行知识图谱引导的有害提示生成，以系统性地生成与领域相关的提示，然后应用双策略模糊重写，将显性有害提示通过直接和上下文增强重写转化为隐性变体。该框架生成的高质量数据集结合了强领域相关性和隐性，能够实现更现实的红队测试，并推动LLM安全研究。我们将在GitHub上发布我们的代码和数据集。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the safety risks posed by large language models (LLMs) in specialized domains like finance and healthcare, where existing methods for generating harmful prompts are limited by their reliance on manual construction and focus on explicit threats. The proposed approach, StealthGraph, differs by utilizing a knowledge-graph-guided framework to systematically generate domain-relevant harmful prompts and employing obfuscation rewriting techniques to convert explicit prompts into implicit ones, thus enhancing their realism and relevance. This contribution not only provides high-quality datasets that reflect real-world threats but also supports more effective red-teaming efforts in LLM safety research. The methodology involves generating prompts through knowledge graphs and rewriting them to improve implicitness, resulting in datasets that better capture the complexities of domain-specific risks and ultimately advance the field of LLM safety.
本研究关注大型语言模型（LLMs）在金融和医疗等专业领域带来的安全风险，现有的有害提示生成方法有限，通常依赖手动构建，主要集中在显性提示上，这些提示容易被检测到。所提出的方法StealthGraph通过利用知识图谱指导生成隐性有害提示，创造出与领域相关且更能反映现实威胁的提示，从而克服了将领域知识转化为可操作约束和增强提示隐性这两个挑战。本文贡献了一个端到端框架，生成具有强领域相关性和隐性的高质量数据集，促进了更现实的红队测试和LLM安全研究的进展。该方法论包括通过知识图谱生成有害提示，并采用模糊重写策略，成功提高了创建支持增强LLM安全目标的数据集的性能。
Uncovering Logit Suppression Vulnerabilities in LLM Safety Alignment
Authors: Yuxi Li, Yi Liu, Yuekang Li, Ling Shi, Gelei Deng, Shengquan Chen, Kailong Wang
First: 2024-05-20T17:17:55+00:00 · Latest: 2026-04-18T08:25:50+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Large language models (LLMs) have revolutionized various applications, making robust safety alignment essential to prevent harmful outputs. Current safety alignment techniques, however, harbor inherent vulnerabilities due to their reliance on logit suppression. In this work, we identify critical logit-level vulnerabilities by introducing Semantic-sensitive Alignment and Generation (SSAG), a method designed to systematically manipulate output-layer logits without altering model parameters. Experiments on five popular LLMs show that SSAG exposes harmful responses with a 95% success rate while reducing response time by 86%. VulMine also demonstrates superior attack efficacy, achieving an average ASR of up to 77% against strong defensive mechanisms. These findings reveal crucial weaknesses in existing alignment methods, highlighting an urgent need for improved vulnerability detection and robust safety alignment strategies. Our code is available on github.
中文标题/摘要
标题：揭示大型语言模型安全对齐中的Logit抑制漏洞
大型语言模型（LLMs）在各种应用中引发了革命，使得强健的安全对齐变得至关重要，以防止有害输出。然而，当前的安全对齐技术由于依赖于Logit抑制，存在固有的漏洞。在本研究中，我们通过引入语义敏感对齐与生成（SSAG）方法，识别出关键的Logit级别漏洞，该方法旨在系统性地操控输出层Logit而不改变模型参数。在五个流行的LLM上进行的实验表明，SSAG以95%的成功率揭示了有害响应，同时将响应时间减少了86%。VulMine还展示了卓越的攻击效果，在强防御机制下实现了高达77%的平均攻击成功率（ASR）。这些发现揭示了现有对齐方法中的关键弱点，突显了改进漏洞检测和强健安全对齐策略的迫切需求。我们的代码可在github上获取。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the critical need for robust safety alignment in large language models (LLMs) to prevent harmful outputs, as existing techniques are vulnerable due to their dependence on logit suppression. Previous methods have not adequately identified these vulnerabilities, prompting the introduction of a novel approach called Semantic-sensitive Alignment and Generation (SSAG), which manipulates output-layer logits without changing model parameters. This approach is well-motivated as it systematically uncovers logit-level weaknesses, contributing to the understanding of safety alignment in LLMs. The methodology involves testing SSAG on five popular LLMs, revealing a 95% success rate in exposing harmful responses and an 86% reduction in response time, along with an average attack success rate of 77% against strong defenses. These results underscore the urgent need for enhanced vulnerability detection and more effective safety alignment strategies in LLMs.
本研究关注大型语言模型（LLMs）中强健安全对齐的迫切需求，以防止有害输出，强调了当前依赖于logit抑制的技术中的脆弱性。以往的方法由于其固有的弱点而显得不足，因此开发了语义敏感对齐与生成（SSAG）方法，该方法在不改变模型参数的情况下操控输出层logits。本文的贡献在于揭示了重要的logit级脆弱性，并证明SSAG能够以95%的成功率暴露有害响应，同时将响应时间减少86%。该方法论涉及对五种流行LLMs的logits进行系统性操控，平均攻击成功率达到77%，从而支持了对改进安全对齐策略的需求。
ReGA: Model-Based Safeguard for LLMs via Representation-Guided Abstraction
Authors: Zeming Wei, Chengcan Wu, Meng Sun
First: 2025-06-02T15:17:38+00:00 · Latest: 2026-04-18T05:40:12+00:00
Comments: FSE 2026
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Large Language Models (LLMs) have achieved tremendous success in various tasks, yet concerns about their safety and security have emerged. In particular, they pose risks of generating harmful content and are vulnerable to jailbreaking attacks, creating unaddressed security issues regarding their deployments. In the context of software engineering for artificial intelligence (SE4AI) techniques, model-based analysis has demonstrated notable potential for analyzing and monitoring machine learning models, particularly in stateful deep neural networks. However, it suffers from scalability issues when extended to LLMs due to their vast feature spaces. In this paper, we aim to address the scalability issue of model-based analysis techniques for safeguarding LLM-scale models. Motivated by the recent discovery of low-dimensional safety-critical representations that emerged in LLMs, we propose ReGA, a model-based analysis framework with Representation-Guided Abstraction, to safeguard LLMs against harmful prompts and generations. By leveraging safety-critical representations, which are key directions in hidden states that indicate safety-related concepts, ReGA effectively narrows the scalability gap when developing the abstract model for safety modeling. Our comprehensive evaluation shows that ReGA performs sufficiently well in distinguishing between safe and harmful inputs, achieving an AUROC of 0.975 at the prompt level and 0.985 at the conversation level. Additionally, ReGA exhibits robustness to real-world attacks and generalization across different safety perspectives, outperforming existing safeguard paradigms in terms of interpretability and scalability. Overall, ReGA serves as an efficient and scalable solution to enhance LLM safety by integrating representation engineering with model-based abstraction, paving the way for new paradigms to utilize software insights for AI safety.
中文标题/摘要
标题：ReGA：基于模型的LLM安全保障通过表示引导抽象
大型语言模型（LLMs）在各种任务中取得了巨大的成功，但也出现了关于其安全性和保密性的担忧。特别是，它们存在生成有害内容的风险，并且容易受到越狱攻击，导致其部署存在未解决的安全问题。在人工智能软件工程（SE4AI）技术的背景下，基于模型的分析在分析和监控机器学习模型方面显示出显著潜力，特别是在有状态的深度神经网络中。然而，当扩展到LLMs时，由于其庞大的特征空间，它面临可扩展性问题。本文旨在解决基于模型的分析技术在保护LLM规模模型时的可扩展性问题。受到最近发现的在LLMs中出现的低维安全关键表示的启发，我们提出了ReGA，一个基于模型的分析框架，采用表示引导抽象，以保护LLMs免受有害提示和生成的影响。通过利用安全关键表示，这些表示是指示安全相关概念的隐藏状态中的关键方向，ReGA有效缩小了在安全建模中开发抽象模型时的可扩展性差距。我们的综合评估表明，ReGA在区分安全和有害输入方面表现良好，在提示级别上实现了0.975的AUROC，在对话级别上实现了0.985。此外，ReGA对现实世界攻击表现出鲁棒性，并在不同安全视角下具有良好的泛化能力，在可解释性和可扩展性方面超越了现有的安全保障范式。总体而言，ReGA作为一种高效且可扩展的解决方案，通过将表示工程与基于模型的抽象相结合，提升LLM安全性，为利用软件洞察力提升AI安全性开辟了新范式。
Summary / 总结
This paper addresses the growing concerns regarding the safety and security of Large Language Models (LLMs), particularly their susceptibility to generating harmful content and vulnerability to jailbreaking attacks. Previous methods in model-based analysis for safeguarding machine learning models have faced scalability challenges when applied to LLMs due to their extensive feature spaces. The proposed approach, ReGA, introduces Representation-Guided Abstraction to effectively manage these scalability issues by utilizing low-dimensional safety-critical representations found in LLMs. The contribution of this research lies in its ability to enhance the safety of LLMs through a model-based framework that improves interpretability and scalability. The methodology involves leveraging safety-critical representations to develop an abstract model for safety modeling, achieving an AUROC of 0.975 at the prompt level and 0.985 at the conversation level, demonstrating robust performance against real-world attacks and generalization across various safety perspectives, thus supporting the goals of improving LLM safety.
本研究关注大型语言模型（LLMs）日益严重的安全性和安全隐患，这些模型容易生成有害内容并且易受越狱攻击。以往的模型分析方法在保护LLMs方面面临可扩展性问题，因为这些模型的特征空间非常广泛。提出的方法ReGA引入了基于表示的抽象，通过利用LLMs中发现的低维安全关键表示，有效解决了可扩展性问题。该方法提高了区分安全和有害输入的能力，在提示级别和对话级别分别达到了0.975和0.985的AUROC，同时在应对现实世界攻击方面表现出强大的鲁棒性，并且在可解释性上优于现有的保护范式。本文的贡献在于提供了一个可扩展且高效的框架，通过结合表示工程和基于模型的抽象来提高LLM的安全性。
SafeDream: Safety World Model for Proactive Early Jailbreak Detection
Authors: Bo Yan, Weikai Lin, Yada Zhu, Song Wang
First: 2026-04-18T04:31:33+00:00 · Latest: 2026-04-18T04:31:33+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Multi-turn jailbreak attacks progressively erode LLM safety alignment across seemingly innocuous conversation turns, achieving success rates exceeding 90% against state-of-the-art models. Existing alignment-based and guardrail methods suffer from three key limitations: they require costly weight modification, evaluate each turn independently without modeling cumulative safety erosion, and detect attacks only after harmful content has been generated. To address these limitations, we first formulate the proactive early jailbreak detection problem with a new metric, detection lead, that measures how early an attack can be detected before the LLM complies. We then propose SAFEDREAM, a lightweight world-model-based framework that operates as an external module without modifying the LLM's weights. SAFEDREAM introduces three components: (1) a safety state world model that encodes LLM hidden states into a compact safety representation and predicts how it evolves across turns, (2) CUSUM detection that accumulates weak per-turn risk signals into reliable evidence, and (3) contrastive imagination that simultaneously rolls out attack and benign futures in latent space to issue early alarms before jailbreaks occur. On three multi-turn jailbreak benchmarks (XGuard-Train, SafeDialBench, SafeMTData) against 8 baselines, SAFEDREAM achieves the best detection timeliness across all benchmarks (1.06-1.20 turns before compliance) while maintaining competitive false positive rates and outperforming baselines in detection quality.
中文标题/摘要
标题：SafeDream：主动早期越狱检测的安全世界模型
多轮越狱攻击逐步侵蚀大型语言模型（LLM）的安全对齐，在看似无害的对话轮次中成功率超过90%，针对最先进的模型。现有的基于对齐和护栏的方法存在三个主要局限性：需要昂贵的权重修改，独立评估每个轮次而不建模累积的安全侵蚀，并且仅在生成有害内容后检测攻击。为了解决这些局限性，我们首先用一个新的指标——检测提前量，来制定主动早期越狱检测问题，该指标衡量攻击在LLM遵从之前可以多早被检测到。然后我们提出SAFEDREAM，一个轻量级的基于世界模型的框架，作为外部模块运行而不修改LLM的权重。SAFEDREAM引入三个组件：（1）安全状态世界模型，将LLM的隐藏状态编码为紧凑的安全表示，并预测其在轮次中的演变；（2）CUSUM检测，将每轮的微弱风险信号累积为可靠证据；（3）对比想象，同时在潜在空间中展开攻击和良性未来，以在越狱发生之前发出早期警报。在三个多轮越狱基准（XGuard-Train、SafeDialBench、SafeMTData）上，SAFEDREAM在所有基准中实现了最佳检测及时性（在遵从之前1.06-1.20轮），同时保持竞争性的误报率，并在检测质量上优于基线。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the growing concern of multi-turn jailbreak attacks that compromise the safety alignment of large language models (LLMs), achieving over 90% success rates against current models. Previous methods, including alignment-based and guardrail approaches, face significant limitations such as the need for expensive weight modifications, independent evaluation of each conversation turn without considering cumulative effects, and delayed detection of harmful content. The proposed SAFEDREAM framework overcomes these issues by introducing a proactive early jailbreak detection metric called detection lead, which measures the timing of attack detection, and operates as an external module without altering LLM weights. SAFEDREAM comprises a safety state world model, CUSUM detection for aggregating risk signals, and contrastive imagination for predicting potential outcomes, resulting in superior detection timeliness across three multi-turn jailbreak benchmarks while maintaining competitive false positive rates and enhancing detection quality compared to eight baseline methods.
本研究关注日益严重的多轮越狱攻击，这些攻击破坏了大型语言模型（LLM）的安全对齐，成功率超过90%。以往的方法，包括基于对齐和护栏的策略，存在显著局限性，如需要昂贵的权重修改、对每个对话轮次的独立评估以及对有害内容的延迟检测。所提出的SAFEDREAM框架通过引入一种不改变LLM权重的主动早期越狱检测策略，采用新的指标检测提前量来衡量攻击的早期检测。SAFEDREAM的贡献包括用于编码LLM状态的安全状态世界模型、用于累积风险信号的CUSUM检测以及用于预测潜在结果的对比想象。在三个多轮越狱基准测试中，SAFEDREAM展示了优越的检测及时性，能够在合规之前1.06到1.20轮次内识别攻击，同时保持竞争性的假阳性率，并在检测质量上优于八种基线方法。
SafeLM: Unified Privacy-Aware Optimization for Trustworthy Federated Large Language Models
Authors: Noor Islam S. Mohammad, Uluğ Bayazıt
First: 2026-04-17T18:00:58+00:00 · Latest: 2026-04-17T18:00:58+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly deployed in high-stakes domains, yet a unified treatment of their overlapping safety challenges remains lacking. We present SafeLM, a framework that jointly addresses four pillars of LLM safety: privacy, security, misinformation, and adversarial robustness. SafeLM combines federated training with gradient smartification and Paillier encryption for privacy, integrates defenses against training and inference-time attacks, employs contrastive grounding with calibrated decoding to reduce hallucinations, and introduces alignment-aware binarized aggregation to enhance robustness while maintaining bounded reconstruction quality. Across benchmarks on factuality, toxicity, and membership inference, SafeLM achieves 98.0% harmful content detection accuracy, reduces communication by 96.9%, and lowers gradient inversion PSNR from 31.7 dB to 15.1 dB. Ablations show that each component contributes independently, whereas their integration yields a strong privacy utility efficiency trade-off for deploying trustworthy LLMs.
中文标题/摘要
标题：SafeLM：可信联邦大型语言模型的统一隐私意识优化
大型语言模型（LLMs）越来越多地应用于高风险领域，但对其重叠安全挑战的统一处理仍然缺乏。我们提出了SafeLM，一个共同解决LLM安全四大支柱的框架：隐私、安全、错误信息和对抗鲁棒性。SafeLM结合了联邦训练、梯度智能化和Paillier加密以保护隐私，整合了针对训练和推理时攻击的防御，采用对比基础与校准解码以减少幻觉，并引入对齐感知的二值聚合以增强鲁棒性，同时保持有限的重构质量。在事实性、有害性和成员推断的基准测试中，SafeLM实现了98.0%的有害内容检测准确率，减少了96.9%的通信量，并将梯度反演PSNR从31.7 dB降低到15.1 dB。消融实验表明，每个组件独立贡献，而它们的整合为部署可信的LLMs提供了强大的隐私效用效率权衡。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the increasing deployment of large language models (LLMs) in critical applications, highlighting the need for a comprehensive approach to their safety challenges, including privacy and security. Previous methods have often tackled these issues in isolation, leading to inefficiencies and vulnerabilities. The proposed SafeLM framework differs by integrating federated training with advanced techniques such as gradient smartification and Paillier encryption, while also incorporating defenses against various attacks and improving factual accuracy through contrastive grounding. This unified approach enhances the robustness of LLMs and ensures a balance between privacy and utility. The methodology demonstrates significant performance improvements, achieving 98.0% accuracy in harmful content detection, a 96.9% reduction in communication costs, and a notable decrease in gradient inversion PSNR, thus supporting the goal of deploying trustworthy LLMs effectively.
本研究关注大型语言模型（LLMs）在高风险环境中的日益应用，强调对其安全挑战（包括隐私、安全、错误信息和对抗鲁棒性）进行统一处理的必要性。以往的方法往往孤立地解决这些问题，导致全面安全解决方案的缺失。提出的SafeLM框架通过将联邦训练与先进的隐私措施（如梯度智能化和Paillier加密）相结合，同时整合对各种攻击的防御措施和减少幻觉的技术，展现出其独特性。这种方法的动机明确，旨在增强LLMs的整体安全性和可信度。该方法论结合了这些创新技术，结果表明SafeLM在有害内容检测中实现了98.0%的准确率，通信成本减少了96.9%，梯度反演PSNR显著降低，表明其性能达到了提高LLMs安全性和效率的目标。
